Agent: `/root/venus_builder`, parent `/root`. Branch `agent/venus`, looks branch `agent/venus-looks`. Times are UTC.

After a compaction: read `assets-src/shared/STOP-AGENT.md`, then this file, then carry on.

## Standing facts

- VM: 4 cores, 7 GB RAM, about 17 GB disk. `npm ci` done, `npm run build` passes.
- Segments (s): hold 37 to 42, dive 42 to 45, terrain 45 to 51, rise 51 to 53.5, travel 53.5 to 56. Judged frames: hold 1185, dive 1287, terrain 1440, rise 1586.
- Budgets: full 1400 MB texture memory with mipmaps, web 450 MB. Disk full 150 MB, web 35 MB (Builder's mail 03:07, the brief said 40). File limit 95 MB. 6 takes per asset per round. 3 rounds with the critics.
- Fit (`src/stops/venus/fit.json`): vfov 30, camera [-0.10884, -0.005645, 4.527276], sunDir [-0.84145, 0.383012, 0.381135], globe quaternion [0.3397865, -0.0671923, -0.1502475, 0.9259892], hero disc cx 1482.82, cy 788.05, r 669.75 in 2816 x 1584. North pole on the painted vortex near px (1649, 302). Sub camera lat 40, lon 0. Sub solar lon -74.8, lat 19.2. Limb residual RMS 1.7 px, sectors 0 and 330 deg off by 5 to 8 px.
- Map convention: dir(lon, lat) = (cos lat sin lon, sin lat, cos lat cos lon), u = lon / 360 + 0.5, north at top.
- Sky patch aims come from `src/space/skyframe.json` `patches.<stop>`, a shared file written by `npx tsx tools/assets/skyframe.ts --keep` (the Builder's). The director computes aims for every plan (`aimSky`), the engine reads `director.sky.patches[id][view]`, so a venus patch is aimed without a skyframe entry.

## Plan of record

1. Orbit view: 2x upscale of hero, unproject on fitted sphere, delight, far side painted at dive patch detail (tiles, poles on polar projection), hero pixels on the visible side faded toward the limb, light curve measured, fitted haze layers plus one across the disc edge, cloud band flow layer, night side lightning, hero sky as sky patch.
2. Terrain view: relief of `venus-close.jpg` (upscale 5632, depth, camera fit, layers for cloud towers, aprons past every edge, haze drift, shafts sway), own sky patch.
3. Dive and rise: patch levels at most 2x apart with predictor, bright haze cover, level rise, far side and poles checked from the rise angle.
4. Self check: measure, compare at final, sheets at review, crops. Then READY.

My `texels.sh` had counted under 2.0 (red byte not saturated), twice as strict. ## Dive and rise (05:50 onward)

- Predictor: `npx tsx assets-src/venus/patches/predict.ts --levels "90,53.13,28.07,14.25" --kinds dive,rise --every 3 [--stop mars] [--width 1920]`. It casts the film camera's rays and repeats the shader's density math. At 1920 x 1080 it gives 0 soft on the Mars dive and rise (as the Mars record says), so it is trusted.
- Spot in the body frame: lon 25.49, lat 50.37 (`patches/spot.ts` writes `patches/spot.json`). Sun at the spot: elevation 8.4 deg, azimuth -70 deg from north (upper left in a north-up patch). Dive heading: azimuth -100 deg (west, toward the Sun).

- 07:05 final stills of 1568 and 1570 (132 s each on this VM): the fragments are not a sampling fault of the review tier only. The bright cumulus tops of the patches blur into bars with hard ends (box shutter), and at 64 samples a fine comb is still visible at native size where the picture moves fastest (lower right of 1570). R4 says: "Fast motion is a smooth blur. It never breaks into separate ghost copies of an edge." Ladder in `kit/04-LOOK.md` "Fast motion breaks into ghost copies": rung 2 is blur along the motion inside each sample.
- 07:10 to 07:19 motion taps in the stop's own shader (`globe.ts`, `set.ts`, `look.ts motion`): in dive and rise frames of the orbit view with more than one sample, `set.ts` takes the plan's pose at the sample time and one sample interval later, measures how far nine surface points slide on screen (`slide`), and when that is over 1.25 px gives the globe shader `uTaps` (one per px, at most 12) and the next sample's camera in the mesh frame (`uCamObj`, `uNextCam`, `uNextRot`, with the change of lens). The shader finds where this pixel's ray meets the sphere now and one sample later and averages the wrap and patch reads along that path, centred on the sample. So the blur never grows past the shutter, it only fills the gaps between samples. Light, haze, limb and lightning are computed once.
  - Off (draft, hold, travel, terrain): draft stills of 1185 and 1305 with the old and the new shader on the same engine are identical (max difference 0). The differences against the stills from before the merge (0.14 percent of values, by 1) are the engine's rounding.
  - On: review still of 1570 (16 samples) is smoother than the final still (64 samples) without taps: `out/work/venus/strip/crop-1570-cmp.png` (review before, final before, review with taps). Review still time at 1568: 80 s (50 s before).
- 07:20 `npx tsx assets-src/venus/motion/slide.ts` prints, per frame, how far the picture slides during one shutter (px at 1920, median and most over the probes) and the cover amount. Dive: 1287 median 2.5 (most 3.1), 1296 7.4, 1299 10.8, 1302 15.9, 1305 21.3 (most 55.5), cover from 1306 (0.01), 1307 0.29, 1308 0.71, 1309 0.99, peak 1311 median 115. Rise: 1562 74.5 (cover 0.99), 1564 47 (0.90), 1566 32.5 (0.73), 1568 33.7 (0.53, most 98.8), 1570 39.9 (0.32), 1572 37.7 (0.14), 1575 28.9 (0), 1578 19.5, 1580 12.0, 1582 8.5, 1584 5.3, 1586 3.3 (most 4.8), 1588 1.8, 1590 0.9, then under 0.5.
- 07:22 to 07:32 dive 1305 (review, 16 samples) with the taps still showed ladder rungs across every streak on the night side (`out/work/venus/strip/crop-1305-zz.png`). Cause found by a test: one sample blurred over the whole shutter by the taps alone (draft, taps forced on, `x-1305.png`) gives the same bars in length and direction with no rungs, so the tap geometry is right; the rungs are the engine's sub pixel jitter (`camera.setViewOffset`, up to half a pixel per sample in any direction), which shifts each sample's short streak sideways. Fix in the stop's shader, only on the tap path: the cloud lookup is taken at the unjittered pixel (`uJitter` from `view.camera.view.offsetX/Y`, `p0 = vLocal - jx dFdx(vLocal) + jy dFdy(vLocal)`); edges (limb, haze, sky) keep the jitter. Result `crop-1305-zz4.png`: continuous streaks.
- The taps are now few and long: each tap reads the wrap and the patches once with an anisotropic footprint stretched along its piece of the path (`stretch()`: `textureGrad` with the major axis along the motion, textures have anisotropy 16), `look.motion = { from: 1, step: 6, most: 8 }` (one tap per 6 px of slide per sample). Same picture as one tap per pixel (mean difference 0.13 of 255), review still 57 s instead of 80 s. The whole tap path sits in a 0 or 1 loop. Off state checked again: draft 1185 and 1305 identical to the stills of commit a098240 (max difference 0).
- At final (64 samples) the taps switch on only where the slide passes 64 px per shutter, that is inside the covers and at 1568 to 1570 in the corners.

Draft frames 1566 to 1571 showed the fault at native size: the cover's shell [1.015, 1.07] is flat out to 1.015 and ends at 1.07, so a cream band with its own outer edge stood outside the limb, with the limb line inside it (a doubled edge), the same all round, also on the night side. And the sky above the horizon went from haze to black in two frames (1560 to 1562), because the camera leaves a shell 0.07 thick that fast (altitudes: 1560 0.027, 1561 0.046, 1562 0.076, 1563 0.119, 1564 0.178, 1565 0.258, 1566 0.362, 1567 0.49, 1568 0.644; dive 1310 0.382, 1311 0.216, 1312 0.093, 1313 0.038).

Near the deck the shell is [1, 1.18] (a gradient from the limb outward, no flat part), far away it is [0.985, 1.045] (falls off across the limb, inside the planet's own glow), blended over altitude 0.26 to 0.65. Same function on the dive and the rise.
- First try (`strip/shell1.jpg`): shell growing with altitude (0.105 + 0.73 h, inner at 20 percent). Wrong: the sky stayed fully veiled to 1564 and cleared in two frames at 1565 to 1567, the pop only moved. The sky pixels in frame are those just above the limb, their distance from the centre is what counts (top of frame: 1.057 at 1562, 1.11 at 1564, 1.146 at 1565, 1.205 at 1566), so a constant shell of about 1.18 gives an even fade from 1561 to 1566.

The stairs are missing anti-aliasing: samples are averaged before the tone curve, a bright disc pixel beside a dim glow pixel averages to a step. Night side at -45: hero rim peak 200 about 5 px wide and its edge about 3 px (1920 scale) further out; film peak 169, a 1 px line. Stars inside the inner glow are in the hero too.
- Engine facts: patch weight is smoothstep(u, 0.2, 0.8) for the arriving stop during travel, 1 in hold and dive, 0 in the rise orbit view. Replace mode `mix(col, inverseFilmic(t) * gain * uGlow * uExposure / vignette, weight)`. Soft rectangle `smoothstep(0, edge, min(e.x, e.y))`, `edge = margin / (1 + 2 * margin)` (`set.ts` about line 90), texture without mips. Drawn star points are switched off by the patch weight. Patch aim = camera at the judged hold frame. Patches are drawn vignette free, the live sky gets the post vignette (0.67 to 0.85 at frame edges). `--format uhd` 3840 x 2160; `--diag ungraded` = toSrgb(hdr) before exposure, bloom, vignette; `--diag nograin`.
- Sky aim conventions measured: tilt + makes the band rise to the right, lift + moves the band down. Hero band line (2816 x 1584): y = 0.0706 x + 640.1, 4.04 deg down to the right, 1.0 deg above the frame centre, about 250 px (4.7 deg) wide. So tilt -4, lift -1 puts the live band on it. The live band is about 15 deg wide, blue grey with pink knots (hero band narrow, warm cream: live 22/25/29 where hero 54/39/26). ### Item 2, the covers (10:40 onward)

- Cover engine facts: `src/terrain/cover.ts` draws its own horizon band (`uLow` below, `uColor` above), darkens above the horizon by `uTop` over 0.22 tan units, alpha from the amount (1 above 0.93), cut by the shell (distance of the ray from the planet centre). `film.ts` order per frame: `coverAmount`, `coverSide`, colours, `coverTop`, `horizon()`, `coverShell`. `coverSide` blends over the middle half of the 16 frame window (dive 0 to 1, rise 1 to 0).
- Diagnosis, rise (review cover sheet of 09:31): dark sky above the horizon 1555 to 1558, all cream 1559 to 1563, black sky from 1564: dark, bright, dark reads as a cut. Cause: `coverTop` 0.85 on the orbit side made the cover's own sky cream. The rise's horizon itself is carried (tilt 0 to 1564, height in frame 0.13 at 1559, 0.141 at 1560, rising smoothly).
- Diagnosis, dive: the engine's swoop turns the limb 15 to 19 degrees per frame from 1306 to 1313 (tilt -74.5, -63.8, -54.2, -38.6, -19.2, -2.0) and stops dead at 0 on the terrain side at 1314.
- Fix in `plan.ts` only: `coverTop: [0.1, 0.12]` (order orbit side, terrain side; was 0.85 on the orbit side), so the sky above the cover's horizon stays dark on both sides. `VenusPlan.dive()` override: from frame 1288 the limb's tilt in frame is re-timed (Hermite from the engine's tilt and slope at 1288 to the terrain entry's tilt and slope at the cover centre), and from 1302 the limb's height in frame as well, by a corrective rotation of the camera only (roll about the view axis, then pitch); position and lens untouched, frames up to 1287 unchanged. `terrain.entryRollDeg: -30` carries the bank into the terrain view and unwinds by 1350.
- `npx tsx assets-src/venus/motion/horizon.ts` prints per frame around both covers: view, altitude, vfov, limb tilt, height in frame, cover amount, side, shell. After the override: tilt -88.0 (1288), -82.7 (1292), -72.3 (1296), -58.9 (1300), -44.6 (1304), -37.8 (1306), -28.8 (1309), -23.9 (1311), -20.3 (1313), -19.0 (1314, terrain), -14.6 (1318), -10.9 (1322), -6.4 (1328), -4.6 (1331), 0 at 1350: at most 3.5 degrees per frame, no stop. Height in frame 1.17 (1303), 1.017 (1306), 0.740 (1309), 0.568 (1311), 0.461 (1313), 0.446 (1314), 0.440 (1315).
- 10:50 draft dive strip with the override (`out/work/venus/strip/dive3.jpg`): the limb rolls evenly from 1292, the dark sky wedge in the upper left stays through the whole cover (curved limb at 1309, straight banked band 1312 to 1316) and becomes the terrain's sky; the banked horizon levels by 1331 with the Sun on it; no hole at the bottom corners of the rolled entry (1314 to 1331).
- 10:58 draft rise strip (`strip/rise3.jpg`): level horizon band with a dark top from 1556 to 1562 (was all cream 1559 to 1563), the top goes from dark olive to black over 1562 to 1565 while the horizon bends into the limb, the globe is clear by 1568. No dark, bright, dark any more.

### Item 5, cloud detail (10:45 onward)

- Orange blocks on the bright cumulus edges (seen at 1185 and 1287 in native crops, the hero is cream there). Cause measured: the wrap stores albedo up to `TOP` 24 and scales brighter texels down with their hue kept. The sunlit cumulus tops on the night side need up to 135 (hero scene value 4.97 clipped in red, light curve there 0.042, 0.071, 0.108): stored 24, 6, 0.6 for a true 125, 31, 3.3. Five times too dim in scene light, and under the tone curve that is orange where the hero is nearly white. Not a JPEG fault (all maps are 4:4:4).
- Fix: the missing factor is stored. `wrap/lift.py` writes log2(true peak / 24) / 3 into the green channel of `wrap-flow.jpg` (red stays the flow layer; 0.019 percent of the map lifted, median 1.9, all with cap weight over 0.42, so nothing of the hero's limb glow). `globe.ts`: `lin *= exp2(max(g - floor, 0) * 3 * smoothstep(0.4, 0.8, texel peak))` (`look.lift`). The gate on the texel's own brightness keeps the soft lift map from brightening dark cloud beside a highlight in the patches. No new texture, no more memory.
- Black single pixels on the limb line at 1287 (draft): the globe mesh is a polygon (320 segments), its silhouette lies up to 4.8e-5 radii inside the sphere, the rim shader discards exactly inside the sphere: a gap of up to 0.026 px at 540 px radius, so about 2.6 percent of limb samples saw the sky between the two. Fix: the mesh is drawn 1 / cos(pi / seg) + 2e-4 larger and the globe shader discards rays that miss the unit sphere (same test as the rim, at the end of main so derivatives stay defined). The "dotted dark line" seen at review at -70 and -20 degrees was this, not the sharpen.
- Thin orange arcs near the grazing limb (vortex side, also 1287 upper right): streaks of the generated map in the outer 9 px, foreshortened to one pixel and kept sharp by anisotropic filtering. Fix: `look.graze = { most: 4, from: 0.32, to: 0.06 }`: the texture footprint of the wrap and the patches grows by up to 5 times as the view angle cosine falls from 0.32 to 0.06, as thick haze would do.
- Lightning: the painted bolt texture was read with an implicit derivative inside a branch (undefined mip level). Now `textureGrad` with the derivatives of the plane coordinate.
- Rise 1586 "combed, embossed strokes": the flow layer is drawn as veil(now) / veil(hold middle). By the rise the two are 12 degrees apart, so the map's own streaks are cancelled and a displaced copy laid over them: a double pattern. Fix in `set.ts`: after the terrain view the flow layer's clock is counted from the judged rise frame (`look.drift.riseAt` 0.75 of the rise), so the veil is neutral at 1586 and drifts around it. The globe is hidden by the covers and the terrain view in between, so nothing jumps in view. The band drift (the map's own shear) is unchanged and continuous.
- Item 6, terrain: `terrain.forward` 0.6 to 1.2 (the judged frame 1440 is the middle of the move and does not change).
- 11:00 results of the test job (`out/work/venus/r2/t1-*.png`): cumulus tops cream at 1185 and 1287 (crops in looks), no black limb pixels at 1287 in that take, 1586 neutral veil. Pushed as fbbb040 with the binaries of round 2 so far. Engine batch 11 merged (fa0cb77): sample count follows screen motion.
- `terrain.forward` 1.2 showed the near edge of the relief as a flat brown strip along the bottom of 1350 (60 px), 0.95 still 10 px; 0.85 is clean at 1338, 1344, 1350 (`r2/f85-*.png`). So forward is 0.85 (was 0.6 in round 1). Share of texels under 1.0 in the terrain view stays 0.
- Where the 1.8 percent of mean level over the hero sits (review 1185): disc inside 400 px +0.6 percent, sky +3 percent (0.5 of 255), halo ring 456 to 560 px +6.4 percent. Edge by steepest slope: film 457 to 459 px all round, hero 454 to 455 at top and bottom and 458 to 462 left and right. The hero's disc is 0.8 percent flattened, the film's is round and 3 px larger at top and bottom, where the limb is brightest. Not chased (a flattened globe would break the analytic limb).
- Limb ring arcs, second pass. `look.graze = { most: 3, from: 0.45, to: 0.2 }` softened the arcs at the hold only a little, and at 1287 the red broken strokes sit further inside the limb (the dive camera sees the hero's limb ring less steeply). Cause in the map itself: `/tmp/limbzone.png` (the wrap relit in the hero's view, without haze) shows red streaks in the ring where the hero is a smooth golden band; taking the limb glow out of the hero and dividing by a light curve near zero left amplified streaks. Fix in the asset, `wrap/calm.py`: in the ring of hold view cosine -0.02 to 0.44 (weight 1 from 0.26 down) the stored value is pulled toward its local mean in log space (value = base * (value / base)^k, k down to 0.15, base = Gaussian of 1 degree). Applied to `wrap-color.jpg` and `patch-a.jpg` of both sets from the backups in `out/work/venus/r2/bak/` (19 percent of the wrap, 17 percent of patch a; patch b, c, d lie outside). Result: review 1185 against the hero in the ring 395 to 452 px 5.98 (before 6.15), whole frame 4.82 (4.83); the arcs are gone at 1185 and 1287 (`/tmp/vortex-crop6.png`, `/tmp/c1287-e.png`).
- Black limb pixels came back at draft 1287 in another take (one or two per frame): the globe and the rim each decide "inside the sphere" from their own interpolated ray, and the two differ in the last bits. Fix: the rim no longer decides. It is depth tested against the globe (back faces of its 2.6 radius shell, so always behind the globe; the orbit group holds nothing else that writes depth) and clamps b to at least 1. A pixel is the globe's or the rim's, never neither and never both.
- The two pink points beside the vortex (1185 near 1300, 200; larger at 1287): not lightning (the cell at 32.04, 54.69 set to 0 changed nothing; it is back at 0.9). The wrap's highlight there is pink: albedo 21.9, 3.3, 1.9 relit gives 218, 142, 135 where the hero is 239, 203, 153. Tried in `calm.py`: a hue correction from the hero (in the hero's view the relit wrap against the hero; where the relit map is bright, green and blue of the albedo scaled by the hero's chroma ratio over the map's, 0.8 to 5). It touched 1.7 percent of the hero's pixels and made the hold worse (review 1185 against the hero 4.90 where it was 4.82; 21.8 in the changed pixels where it was 18.1), because the film's highlights are the map plus haze and limb light, not the bare relit map. Switched off (`HUE.gain = (1, 1)`), files rebuilt without it (patch b, c, d byte identical to round 1). The two pink points stay; they are 2 px at the hold and 4 px at 1287.
- 11:20 Builder: at 1618 (travel out, review sheet on main) the far side showed blocky orange squares. Draft 1618 on this build (`out/work/venus/r2/g4-1618.png`): the part the leaving camera sees beside the vortex is the hero's limb ring, the same streaks as the arcs above, seen face on and magnified. With the calmed ring it is a smooth golden band. The globe leaves the frame by about 1630 (1640: stars only).
- 11:25 state after engine batch 12 (merge 0d4513b): draft travel strip 1605 to 1630 (`out/work/venus/strip/travel4.jpg`): the limb glow is whole with the depth tested rim, the band beside the vortex is smooth at 1618 and 1622, the globe is out of frame by 1626. Draft 1287: no arcs, no dark limb pixels, cream cumulus; lower left boosted 5 times shows a soft change of sky tone (the hero's neutral dark sky into the live blue dust) over about 60 px, no line. Draft 1586 beside round 1 (`/tmp/g6-1586-native.png`): the crosshatch over the feathers is gone, the feathers themselves are the hero's.
- Review 1185 against the hero, round 1 then now (mean abs of 255): whole frame 4.70, 4.82; disc inside 395 px 8.46, 8.53; ring 395 to 452 px 6.19, 5.98; halo 456 to 560 px 4.61, 5.28; sky 2.72, 2.73; mean level 52.64, 52.81 (hero 51.88). ## Round 3 work (13:10 onward)

Second render copy: `/home/user/workspace/r2` (git worktree) with `/home/user/workspace/rr.sh NAME COLS TIER FRAME...` (syncs the three venus folders, renders there, writes `out/work/venus/r3/NAME-FRAME.png` and the strip `NAME.jpg`; `NOCOVER=1` switches the veil off in the copy). Sheets, compare, measure, gates and looks run in the main repo.

Diagnosis (13:13 to 13:30):
- Covers rendered without the veil (`out/work/venus/strip/nc-dive.jpg`, `nc-rise.jpg`): the two views already agree closely at both swaps (rise: curved horizon, terrain 1559 at 0.53 of frame height, orbit 1560 at 0.43). What makes two horizons is the engine's straight band and my thick shell (1.18 radii).
- The engine's terrain horizon ignores the camera's height above the deck (true dip at the exit height is 15.1 degrees, the fit's 10.9), so the band and my dive override sat above the visible terrain horizon (dive 1314: engine 0.446 half heights above centre, visible about 0.16).
- Cover shader (`src/terrain/cover.ts`): colour from a straight line, alpha cut by a shell around the planet centre (`coverShell`, orbit) or around a sphere I name (`coverSea`, terrain). The rush pattern only moves radially (`phase = sign * speed * dt`).
- 1287 limb: the violet highlight is a seeded lightning cell on the limb. The orange line is the hero's short bright streak in the hold table. Band and gap: the hold table's veil (alpha 1 at the edge, plateau 0.04 to 26 px inside) over a globe that is brighter than the veil near the limb once the camera has moved. At the hold the film matches the hero there, so the hold table stays for the hold.
- Hold table layout: `limb.bin` float32 RGBA, 65 columns; rows 0 to 127 inside (row 127 the edge, square root mapping over 0.3 radii), rows 128 to 255 outside (1.5 radii).

Decisions and changes (uncommitted at 13:35, see the commit that holds these notes):
- Item 5: lightning off once the dive starts (`look.leave.seconds 0.5`, `left` in `set.ts`); none in 1287 or 1586.
- Item 4: `limb.bin` now has 512 rows. Rows 256 to 511 are a far table (`assets-src/venus/fit/far.py`, from the hold table: azimuth median 9 plus Gaussian 2 cells, night side inside veil one falloff, inside alpha halved on the night side, monotone inside and outside). `uLimbFar = left` mixes hold table to far table in `rim.ts` and `globe.ts`.
- Items 1 and 2: the veil's edge is the limb in both views. `VenusPlan.horizon()` sets `coverShell` (orbit) or `coverSea` (terrain, the relief's own sphere from `relief.json` camera up and radius) to a thin edge, a few thousandths of the limb's angular radius, and returns the true sphere horizon for the terrain view too. Cover colours uniform (`coverTop [1, 1]`, cover = coverLow), so there is no straight band. `veil` replaces `haze`/`shell`. Faster rush.
- Item 3: terrain `side` from -0.1 to about -0.9 (test `r3/ta.jpg` at -0.7: the towers cross a third of the frame against the horizon, near banks pass out at the bottom, no holes at 1350 or 1529).

The shade pass takes its wide sample at `max(shade.grad, grad)` and the weave is divided by the smear, so neither puts crisp detail back.

## Phase 6 polish, item 1: the covers (19:50 onward)

Render copy and scripts as before (`/home/user/workspace/r2`). New: `sync2.sh eng|main` (eng puts the engine files of agent/engine-perf 837adbb into the copy so `coverWindow` works there; never committed), `sheets2.sh TAG TIER eng|main [rise|dive|both]` (both cover sheets, preview format), `strip2.sh TAG TIER eng|main COLS FRAMES` (stills of any frames in one browser session through `assets-src/venus/motion/frames.ts`, strip to `view/TAG.jpg`).

What the engine does (read in `src/director/film.ts`, `src/core/motion.ts`): the sky is dimmed by `(1 - coverAmount)^2`; the engine's cover pass is cut by `coverShell`; the sample count of a fast frame is scaled by `1 - coverAmount * coverHides`; a set's `near` object is drawn after a depth clear, before the engine's cover; `light.skyLow [d0, d1, level]` scales the orbit sky by `mix(level, 1, smooth(ramp(d0, d1, altitude)))` in dive and rise; the terrain sky is the mode `terrainSkyGlow 0.25`. The sky's aim changes at the swap (the engine's rule: the sky changes under a cover), so the star field jumps there in any case.

Design taken (all in my folders):
- The engine's own cover pass draws nothing: `coverShell [-2, -1]` and `coverSea` edge `[-2, -1]`. `coverAmount` is now only a small number (`veil.sky 0.02` times an eased bump), kept above 0 at the centre because the engine tests it; `coverHides 33` keeps the sample factor at 0.34 at the centre as before.
- The veil is my own pass, `veil.ts`, the set's `near`: one full screen triangle pair, rays from the camera's inverse projection (so it follows the jitter and the shutter), masked to the planet's disc (deck sphere in the terrain view, the globe in the orbit view), so it never touches the sky. Blend One, OneMinusSrcAlpha, alpha channel untouched, discarded in the diagnostic views.
- Sheets: four spheres below the camera at altitudes `lowest * step^i` (step 2.5), each with the cloud tile `veil-clouds.jpg` (from patch d, `assets-src/venus/veil/tile.py`, recipe in RECIPE.md) at a tile width of 3 sheet altitudes, the tile turned so its baked light faces the carried Sun. A sheet is born at the camera's altitude, fades in, recedes and fades out, so the pattern is the same at every altitude and streams with the true pull back. Holes from a wide noise plus the tile's luma; `amount` closes them. Sheets above 0.05 to 0.1 radii fade (`ceiling`), so the veil stays in the atmosphere when the camera is high. Lower sheets go into a fog colour that is brighter toward the Sun; at the swap a fog base closes what is left (`solid`).
- The swap is carried: `swapOf` in `set.ts` takes both poses at the swap frame. On the terrain side the sheets are seen by a virtual camera on the orbit view's sphere (turn = orbit quaternion times inverse terrain quaternion, altitude = orbit altitude at the swap times exp(rate * drop), with the rate eased in from zero at the window's edge, `veil.ease 2`). On the orbit side the real camera is used. So the sheets do not jump at the swap.
- The Sun: the terrain's painted air dome (Sun and glare) is carried into the orbit view's sky by `veil.ts` (second block, same texture, same mapping, fixed at the swap) and fades with `(1 - released)^sunFade`; the globe is graded toward the terrain picture (`look.veil.grade`, `uCarry` in `globe.ts`) and released over `veil.carry` 12 frames.
- Timing follows the window: `plan.phase()` gives -1 to 1 across `coverWindow(seg)`, 0 at the swap, so it follows `seconds` and `center`.
- Sky: `light.skyLow [0.03, 1.2, 0.55]` lowers the orbit sky toward the deck (terrain sky mode is 0.25 of the orbit sky); frames above 1.2 radii altitude do not change (1287 is at 2.63, 1586 at 4.21; dive from 1303, rise up to 1571).

Takes seen at draft (strips in `/home/user/workspace/view`, pushed as looks):
- a: noise sheets, cream. Sky, Sun carry, horizon right. Veil a pale wash.
- b: noise sheets with harder edges: flat beige blobs. Rejected; the sheets need a real cloud picture.
- c1: tile sheets, pale. Form reads as cloud banks. Too bright against the terrain (veil 177 against 40 to 130 levels), the terrain is gone 7 frames before the swap, speckle of bright tops when thin.
- c2: dark terrain colours, wide holes, eased rate, sky numbers above. Reads as a brown wrinkled surface (a third surface) and pale flat cut outs high above the globe (sheets at 0.2 radii altitude).
- c3: ceiling, fog base, translucent sheets (`dense 0.8`), Sun glow in the fog.
- c4 (first review tier strip, rise, 1552 to 1570): sky, stars, Sun and horizon continuous in every frame. But 1558 to 1562 are a flat tan wash with faint vertical streaks: the sheets zoom 1.68 times per frame at the swap (the real pull back), each sheet lives about 6 frames, the shutter averages 16 to 128 positions and at full amount every hole is closed, so only the tile's fine detail is left and it averages out. The critic's "wash with no form" again. Draft frames hide this, so the covers are judged at review tier only.
- c5 (draft, both covers, every frame): darker terrain side sheets, fog base from amount 0.55, stronger grade. Structure right, still the fast zoom.
- Decision 20:50: the sheets stream slower than the real pull back (`veil.slow`, the log of the virtual altitude is compressed about the swap on both sides; on the orbit side the ray angle from the nadir is scaled so the sheets' horizon stays on the real limb), the streak is drawn on purpose (taps along the pull back over the true shutter length, `veil.streak`, thresholded after the blur so the shapes are long and keep an edge), and holes stay open at full amount over the fog base, so there is form in every frame.

Item 5 first look (20:40, while a strip rendered): autocorrelation of the star layer in the round 23 final frame 1440, left of the frame above the limb: peak at dx 0, dy 36 (0.04). `sky-terrain.webp` has the same at dy 51 patch px (51 x 1920 / 2816 = 35 film px). The approved image has a weak one at dy 51 too (0.02). So the second copy is inside the terrain sky patch, made in `relief/air.py` (donor fill or mirror), not the shared sky and not the dome. To do: find the step and remove it.
- c6 (review, taps): the streak as 5 texture taps along the pull back per sheet, 16 times anisotropic. Looked right at 1558 (long soft streaks with light and dark groups) but cost 5.4 s per sample at preview format (a 128 sample frame 11 minutes, c4 took 1.5). Dropped.
- c7, c8 (kept design): one texture lookup per sheet. The shapes come from noise drawn in polar coordinates about the point below the camera (angle, log of distance; `look.veil.sheets.fan [12, 1.6, 2.7, 5]`, bent by the cloud noise, `wave 0.2`), mixed in by how fast the real camera pulls back (`plan.veil.rush`: log zoom over the shutter at which the streaks are full, 0.12). In those coordinates a pull back is a slide along the streak, so the streaks keep their place and their texture streams along them. `plan.veil.slow 0.45`: the sheets' virtual altitude is height at the swap times (real altitude over that height) to the power 0.45 on the orbit side, and exp(0.45 rate drop) on the terrain side, so a sheet lives about 15 frames instead of 6; on the orbit side the ray's angle from the nadir is scaled by asin(1/(1+low)) / asin(1/(1+high)) so the sheets end on the real limb. Holes stay open at full amount (`thin 0.9`, linear `ramp 1`) over the fog base (`solid [0.55, 1]`), so no frame is a flat wash. The streaks are in the draft tier too, so draft strips show the veil as it is.
- Window: 1.0 s for both covers (plan `coverWindow`), frames 1299 to 1328 and 1545 to 1574. With 0.8 s the terrain thinned over 6 frames, with 1.0 s over 8 (1549 to 1556) and the globe comes over 8 (1564 to 1571). `skyLow [0.03, 2, 0.55]`: the orbit sky rises from 0.55 to 1 between 0.03 and 2 radii of altitude (frames 1291 to 1314 and 1560 to 1574; 1287 at 2.63 and 1586 at 4.21 are not touched).

Item 5 (21:00): cause found and cured in `relief/air.py` (see RECIPE, "Phase 6, item 5"): the star fill below the line 1 deg above the horizon was a mirror image, so each star near the limb had a twin at twice its distance to the line; at 1440 the twins of the two bright stars at x 206 and 239 stood 36 px below them on the limb's edge. Now a shifted copy from 300 px higher, faded in 8 to 64 px below the line. `relief-air.png` unchanged (same md5). To check: 1440 at draft against the round 23 frame, then the frames where the horizon dips (1529, 1559).

## Phase 6 polish, state at 22:35 (read this first after a compaction)

Item 1 (covers), draft strips c9 to c12 (`view/c9r.jpg`, `view/c9d.jpg`, `view/c12-1559.jpg`, `view/c12-1306.jpg`), state c12, review sheets rendering since 22:30 (`./sheets2.sh c12 review eng both`, outputs `view/c12-rc.png`, `view/c12-dc.png`):
- Sky level per frame is measured as the linear luma mean of the top twelfth of the rows (`/tmp/skylev.py TAG A B` on strip frames).
- Finding: the dip of the sky after the rise swap did not come from `skyLow`. It came from two things that the terrain picture has and the orbit picture lacks: the engine's wide Sun glare (absent on the orbit side of the swap, the Sun is out of view there) and the glow of the terrain sky patch. Ratio orbit over terrain across the sky was 0.5 to 0.8.
- Cure in my folders: the carried air dome (the terrain Sun and its glare, drawn by `veil.ts` on the orbit side) is lifted by `look.veil.sunLift 0.4` while it is carried and compressed as it fades (`sunSoft 6`: `kept = 1 / (1 + sunSoft * (1 - weight) * luma)`, `glow = mix(luma * sunTint, glow, kept) * kept`), so the Sun leaves as a soft warm glow and not as a hard disc. The teal core of the fading Sun in take c10 came from hue blown up by the inverse filmic; `sunTint [1.15, 0.97, 0.75]` takes the fading glow to one warm hue. `skyLow [0.03, 2.4, 0.6]`.
- Numbers at draft, c12: rise 1559 0.0088, 1560 0.0074, 1561 0.0067, 1562 0.0082, 1563 0.0111, 1564 0.0158 (take c9: 0.0053 and 0.0049 at 1560 and 1561). Smoothed ratio orbit 1560 over terrain 1559 across the sky 0.63 to 1.25, mostly 0.85 to 1.1. Dive 1306 to 1313 0.0144 to 0.0199, 1314 0.0149; the level before the cover is 0.0139.
- `skyLow` reaches to 2.4 radii of altitude now: 1287 (2.63) and 1586 (4.21) are still outside it; to check with stills before the report.
- Engine asks for the report: (a) the engine's own cover pass costs 0.07 to 0.15 s per sample although `coverShell` hides it; (b) the star field jumps at the swap frame because the engine turns the sky there and my veil no longer covers the sky: the engine would have to turn the sky over the cover window or cross fade the two skies; (c) the band of the Milky Way is brighter after the rise swap (1563 to 1566): `skyLow` scales stars and band together, a band only factor rising with altitude from `terrainSkyGlow` to 1 would cure it; (d) the engine's Sun glare is absent on the orbit side of the swap (I lift the carried dome by 0.4 instead).

Item 3 (dive 1287), code written at 22:30, not yet seen in a render:
- `uLimbFar` is 1 from frame 1275, so the shade pass was already at full strength at 1287. What the night side lacks there is structure, so the hold's detail layer now stays alive in the dive as a high pass: `globe.ts` takes the gain against a low pass of the same map (`textureLod(uHold, hq, look.hold.far.lod)`, mixed in by `uLimbFar`), so away from the hold only the hero's fine structure is carried and the broad light of the hold is not painted twice.
- Where: inside `look.hold.far.inner [0.86, 0.94]` of the hold's disc radius (the edge part stays hold only), and only where the map is denser than the screen (`dense` = map texels per pixel from the screen gradient of the lookup, weight `smoothstep(1, look.hold.far.dense 1.25, dense)`), and not under the motion taps. `texels = mix(texels, dense, held)` so the texel diagnostic counts the honest density.
- At 1287 the screen has 766 px per radius at the nadir; the 1536 map gives 756 texels per radius (0.99, under 1.0), so the map goes to 2048 (web 1024): 1008 texels per radius (1.32). `detail.py` `SIZE` changed; the float state must be rebuilt (`build` then two `refine` passes), the hold's zones checked against take g3 (nograin 1.95, 0.72, 0.45, 2.96, 2.41).
- `set.ts`: `uHoldLook.x` is 0 in the rise (`ending`), so the rise is not changed by this.
- With the committed 1536 map the layer is off in the dive (density 0.99), so the branch head renders the dive as before until the 2048 map is committed.

Stars and Sun in all twelve frames, the Sun leaves as a soft glow 1563 to 1564, one horizon across 1559/1560, streaks with form in every frame.

Item 4 (rise 1586), studied offline 22:35 to 22:45, code written, not yet seen in a render:
- Geometry: wrap lookups use `direction(lon, lat) = (cos lat sin lon, sin lat, cos lat cos lon)`. Hold camera sub point lon 0, lat 40. Rise 1586 eye at lon 35, lat 55, phase 76 deg. The vertex of the plume's V is at about lon -17, lat 12.5. `assets-src/venus/motion/body.ts FRAME` prints eye, right, up, forward and Sun of a frame in the cloud frame; `/home/user/workspace/proto/bodyview.py BODYJSON OUT [lon lat deg wide]` draws the wrap unlit from that pose, with an optional swirl.
- 4a, the V: a swirl of the lookup direction about an axis, angle `deg * exp(-(dist / wide)^2)`. `globe.ts`: `uSwirl` (axis, angle), `uSwirlWide`, `curl(v)`, applied to the wrap direction, the patch direction and the motion taps; a conditional so that with angle 0 the frame is bit identical. On only after the middle of the terrain segment (`state.time > terrainMid`), so the hold and the dive are not touched. `look.swirl { lon -13, lat 19, deg 130, wideDeg 12 }`. Offline previews `view/bv-apex2.jpg`: the V becomes a curled comma with an eye; +130 turns the same way as the main storm.
- 4c, the blurred bands at the lower right: the cause is in the map. The wrap's calm ring (hold view cosine -0.02 to 0.44, made so the hold's limb is calm) has no cloud detail (high pass energy under 1 against 3 to 6 elsewhere, `view/wrap-all.jpg`), and the rise sees that ring face on across the lower third of the disc (`view/bv-ring.jpg`). A wider footprint cannot put detail into it. First cure: the weave's donor lookup is read at a fixed level (`textureLod(uMap, lonLat(from), uWeave.z)`, `look.weave.base 128`), so the ring takes mid scale cloud structure from the far side donor, the same at any distance. If not enough: bake the transfer into `wrap-color.jpg` and refit the hold layers.
- 4b, the night limb: `tools/limbdip.py` on the round 23 still 1586: dips of 6.8 (az -170), 6.1 (-90), 6.7 (-70), 8.8 (-60), 23.2 (-50), 17.1 (-40), 9.6 (-30) levels, lit side under 4.3. The far table's night model `SHAPE (0.45, 2.5, 0.55, 18)` in `fit/far.py` is in px at 543 px radius; at 1586 the radius is 405 px, so its spike is under 2 px and decays faster than the surface recovers from the haze. Plan: smaller spike, longer tail, maybe lower `VEIL`, then limbdip at 1586 and 1287, target at most 3 levels at both.

Horizon row per frame (`tools/sheethorizon.py`, in my workspace): steps of 1 to 3 cell px per frame through the dive cover, 4 to 5 cell px at the rise swap (1559 to 1560), 1 to 3 around it.
- FAULT OF MINE FOUND BY THE MEASURE CHAIN: `plan.coverHides` was 33 since the veil went in (my trick to keep the sample count low while `coverAmount` is only 0.02). `tests/motion.test.ts` asks for 0 to 1, so vitest failed on agent/venus (12 tests, 1 failed; main passes). Now `coverHides: 1`. Effect: sample counts in the cover's fast frames are at the cap (review 128 in 1304 to 1313 and 1560 to 1571, final 146 to 256), render time up there, the picture can only get smoother. Engine ask (e): a hidden share for the sample rule that does not ride on `coverAmount` (the engine dims the sky by `(1 - coverAmount)^2`, which is why my `coverAmount` is tiny).
- Stars (minor, not done): the film's stars are brighter than the approved images shown at 1920: pixels over 120 in the sky at 1185: film 4097, hero 2848; at 1440 top left, pixels over 100: 592 against 334; dark sky median +0.3 to +0.6 levels. Cause: the patch is 1.47 texels per pixel and is filtered in linear light (GPU sRGB decode, then the sample average in scene light), the reference is averaged in display space; simulated, that gives 3285 to 3453, the rest is bloom. At 1185 the sky also slides 2.1 px per frame, so the shutter blurs stars by 1 px and shifts them 0.6 px (sky zone 2.49 unshifted, 1.55 against the hero shifted 0.6 px). A patch side cure would be to take about 20 percent off the stars' excess over their local median in both patches; it changes the approved sky inside the border, so I left it and report it.

The true Sun sits 20 degrees apart in the two cameras, which is why the glare is carried.

The engine takes two patches, both aimed by the engine, and none on the orbit side of a rise. A dissolve drawn in my veil pass would paint a sky in front of the engine's sky and of the flight stars of batch 19, which mail 71 forbids (do not mask them) and the brief forbids (do not paint against the sky behind the rise). So it stays engine item (b). What the engine would need for a continuous sky: the turn after the swap = camera after x inverse of camera before x turn before, eased to the orbit turn over the rest of the window, with the terrain patch faded out before the swap.

## Stability over consecutive frames (R4), measured 06:15 (22 Nov)

Three consecutive finals of the hold (1185 to 1187) and of the terrain view (1440 to 1442) at master size (`r2/out/work/venus/p6/stf-*.png`; stf-1185 and stf-1440 the same bytes as the judged finals). `tools/stab.py` (in the workspace, not in the repo): phase correlation gives the sky's shift per frame, then every star's peak and its 5 by 5 flux are followed into the next frame.

- Hold: the sky moves 1.97 px right and 0.76 px up per frame, the same in both pairs (the camera drifts, the move is even). 2,746 stars followed: peak ratio median 0.99, half of them change by under 9 percent, a tenth by more than 19 percent; flux half under 2.8 percent, a tenth over 8.5 percent.

What I found at native size (review tier, master size, `r2/out/work/venus/p6/rl-1558.png`, `rl-1314.png`, `rl-1570.png`, `rl-1566.png`):
- 1558 and 1314 (under the cover, terrain side): straight pale bars and soft rectangles 40 to 70 px wide (`view/rl1558-full.jpg`, `view/rl1314-full.jpg`). Two causes, both in my veil (`veil.ts`), none in the engine:
  1. the rush pattern `lines` is value noise on a lattice in fan coordinates (angle, log radius about the camera's foot), thresholded with a narrow band (`edge[1]` 0.25 against a noise swing of 1.2): radial planks with crisp sides.
  2. the veil's sheets come in as small crisp islands of the cloud texture, flat in colour (the deep sheets are 85 percent fog colour): pale cream blobs and flat pink patches lying on the terrain from 1548 to 1556 and 1317 to 1327 (draft, master size: `view/dn-mid.jpg`; the same frame with the veil switched off in the render copy only: `view/nv-1550.jpg`, the terrain below is clean). A compact bright blob smeared by the shutter is the rectangle at 1558.
- 1570 and 1566 (orbit side, 128 samples): smooth, the shreds leave as short streaks; acceptable.
- Early rise 1529 to 1547 at draft, master size (`view/dn-early.jpg`): clean.

Experiments (terrain side frames at draft master size `./e3.sh TAG` gives `view/TAG-r.jpg` 1546 to 1556 and `view/TAG-d.jpg` 1317 to 1327; review native `./e2n.sh TAG` gives `view/TAG-1558.jpg`, `view/TAG-1314.jpg`):
- E2a `fray [6, 0.6, 0.25]` (a strand noise along the angle shifts the log radius of the lattice and adds to it): the planks break into bundles, but as crisp needles (`view/e2a-1558-crop.jpg`). Not enough alone.
- E2b adds `fray[3]` 2: the threshold band widens with the rush (`soft = edge[1] * rush * fray[3]`, centred on the old band). 1558 and 1314 at review native: soft fine streaks, no plank (`view/e2b-1558.jpg`, `view/e2b-1314.jpg`). Kept so far. At rush 0 nothing changes.
- E3a `mist [1, 0.5, 0.9]` (band wider at low amount, terrain side only): no visible change, the islands follow the texture's own hard edges.
- E3b adds `wait [0.5, 0.95]` (the deep sheets, levels 1.1 to 3 below the camera, come in late): fewer patches, blobs still there.
- E3c adds `mist[3]` 16: the cloud texture is read 16 times coarser while the veil is thin (`wide` in `textureGrad`), so the first veil is soft mist, not islands. 1546 to 1554 and 1321 to 1327: mist over a terrain that stays whole (`view/e3c-r.jpg`, `view/e3c-d.jpg`). 1556 still switches to crisp decks, so the range went to `mist [1, 0.7, 1, 16]` (take E3d, review strip `view/e3r.jpg` rendering at 06:49).

- The judged frames must not move: 1185, 1440 have no veil; 1287 and 1586 have amount 0 (check with `./fin.sh TAG` and `cmp` against `f16-*.png` before any commit).
- Not the engine's: the soft squares are smeared veil islands, not coarse mips (`uGrad` never goes above 1).

What the columns were (dive 1319 to 1325, `view/e3n-1321.jpg` at review tier, master size): narrow pale bars with parallel sides at the bottom centre, like smears on glass. Traced in four cheap takes:
- F1 `mist[4]` 1 (the rush pattern is switched off while the veil is thin: `uRush = rush * (1 - thin * mist[4])`): the fine striation goes, a soft pale column and a fan of soft streaks stay (`view/f1-d.jpg`).
- F2 the same with the blur made round by hand (`span` added to both gradient vectors): the same picture as F1 (139 pixels over 4 levels at 1321). The texture has no anisotropic filter, so the blur was round already. Taken out again.
- Veil switched off in the render copy only (`view/nv2-pair.jpg`): the terrain at 1321 and 1325 is clean, so the fan is the veil's.
- Cause: the sheets nearest under the camera. A sheet at 0.3 to 1 levels below the eye lies 0.24 to 0.6 of the eye's height under it, while its forms are 3 to 6 sheet heights wide, so every form is seen at a very flat angle and its sides run as straight lines from the bottom of the frame to the horizon. Under a thick veil nobody sees that; under the thin mist it is the column.
- F3 `mist[5]` 0.7: while the veil is thin the near fade of the sheets starts 0.7 levels deeper (`uFade` set per frame to `near[0] + thin * mist[5]`, `near[1] + thin * mist[5]`), so the mist is carried by the sheets 1 to 3 levels down, which are seen from well above. 1321, 1323, 1325 at review tier, master size: no column, soft mist, the terrain whole (`view/col-1321-pair.jpg`, left E3d, right F3). Rise entry 1546 to 1556 at draft: clean (`view/f3-r.jpg`).

- Kept: F4. Look now `sheets.mist [1, 0.85, 1, 16, 1, 0.7]` (band width, amount from, amount to, coarser texture read, share of the rush pattern removed, levels the near fade moves down), `fray [6, 0.6, 0.25, 2]`, `wait [0.5, 0.95]`.

### What I read in the pictures (crops of main's own finals, `view/k-*.jpg`, no render needed)

- 1287 night side (`k-1287-night.jpg`): the critic is right, it reads as coral. Candidate cause: `look.shade { far 0.8, grad 16, cap 2.5 }` in `globe.ts`, a local contrast gain on the night side that comes in with `uLimbFar` after the hold. Test: `shade.far` 0 and 0.35.
- 1287 dark limb (`k-1287-rlimb.jpg`, `k-1287-lrlimb.jpg`): stars run to the edge at full strength because the Rim's alpha is `uLimbFar * hide.z * smoothstep(0.12, 0.6, luminance of the glow)`, zero on the dim dark limb. The far outside limb table (`limb.bin` rows 384 to 511) is monotonic, so the "banded ridges" are the wrap's grazing bands seen through the far inside table (alpha 0.5 at the edge on the dark azimuths). Tests: star dimming near the limb (`bloom.dim [k, h]`, uniform `uDim` in `rim.ts`), thicker haze over the night limb (`look.dusk { most, from }`, uniform `uDusk` in `globe.ts`).

- 1185 top sunlit limb (`k-1185-top.jpg`, `k-1185-ring.png`): a real deviation from the hero. ### What the two gates were (read in the code, draft strips `view/m-dive-out.jpg`, `view/m-rise-in.jpg`)

- On the terrain side the veil's opaque fog fill (`solid = smoothstep(0.55, 1, amount)`) fell from 0.97 to 0 in five frames (1317 to 1322) and rose in the same way (1553 to 1557): the terrain picture came and went in about three frames. That is the "snap in" and the "smear in one step".
- The veil's streaks died with the amount (`mist[4] = 1`, `thin` 1 below amount 0.85), not with the fall speed: streaks to 1316, flat fog 1317 and 1318, then a sharp terrain. `plan.drop` gives the fall speed (1 minus x, squared): 0.64 at 1317, 0.37 at 1320, 0.18 at 1323, 0.06 at 1326; rise 0.12 at 1550, 0.30 at 1553, 0.56 at 1556. The veil's own rush is clamped at 1 from 1308 to 1320 and 1555 to 1564.
- The terrain picture under the veil was sharp from its first frame, while the veil said "fast".

### The cure (one commit, four files: `veil.ts`, `relief.ts`, `set.ts`, `look.ts`)

1. `look.veil.sheets.solidTerrain [0.25, 1]`: the fog fill on the terrain side opens over amount 1 to 0.25 (frames 1317 to 1326, rise 1549 to 1557). `uSolid` is now a vec3: x, y the fill's range (mixed by the `mist` argument, 0 on the orbit side), z the sheets' `open` top, which stays 0.55 on both sides. The orbit side gets exactly its old numbers.
2. `look.veil.sheets.mist[4]` 1 to 0: on the terrain side the mist keeps the streak pattern in its alpha for as long as the fall speed gives a rush, so the streaks soften and shorten with the speed instead of dying in one frame. `mist` values act on the terrain side only (`thin = mist * ...`).
3. `look.veil.slip 0.15`, `look.relief.slipTaps 12`: under the veil (terrain side, `thick > 0`) the relief layers are drawn with 12 taps along the screen path each point takes when the camera moves along the veil's own fall line (the veil's nadir in the camera, the centre of its streak fan), length = `slip` times the fall speed times the camera's height over the deck, per pixel by depth (near clouds long, horizon none). Colour and alpha both. Outside the veil `uSlip` is 0, `uTaps` 1, `uHeld` 0: one `textureGrad` at the old place with the old gradients, so the judged frames should keep their bytes (to be proved by `cmp` at final tier).
   - This is a blur drawn in my folder. Mail 13 forbids blur tricks that hide the engine's ghost copies in the fastest frames; this one is the handover on the slow side of the cover (the critic's own fix: "tie blur length to camera speed so detail sharpens as the descent eases", "let the blur build with it") and it is named as what it is in the cut message.
   - The terrain move itself is not touched.

### The cure as committed (90cc5b23)

- `relief.ts`: uniforms `uSlip` (vec4: a camera step in view space, w = pixels per unit at depth 1), `uTaps`, `uHeld`. Colour = mean of `uTaps` `textureGrad` taps along the uv path of that step (`along = gx * slip.x + gy * slip.y`), each tap widened to the tap spacing; alpha the same with `uHeld` taps when the layer has an alpha map. `update(..., sky = 1, slip = null)`: with `slip` null the uniforms are 0, 1, 0 and the shader takes one tap at the old place with the old gradients.
- `set.ts`, terrain branch: `thick <= 0` keeps the old calls. Else `slip` = minus the veil's eye direction turned into the camera, times `look.veil.slip * fell * fps / shutterFraction * (|localP| - 1) * deck.radius`, with `fell` the veil's fall inside the shutter (`plan.drop`), and `ground.update(..., 1, slip)`.
- `look.ts`: `veil.slip 0.3`, `relief.slipTaps 12`, `veil.sheets.mist[4]` 1 to 0 (on the terrain side the soft veil keeps its streak pattern as long as the rush lasts).
- Acts on frames 1314 to 1328 and 1546 to 1559 (terrain side with the veil above 0); 1314 to 1316, 1558 and 1559 are fully covered and keep their bytes. All inside the cover windows 1299 to 1328 and 1545 to 1575, which G13 skips. The terrain move, the veil's timing, the orbit side and every frame outside the windows are not touched.
- Mail 13 said: no blur tricks in my folder to hide the engine's ghost copies in the fastest frames. This is a blur in my folder, on the slow side of the cover, along the veil's fall and not along the camera's path. It goes into the cut message in those words, so the Builder can refuse it.

- minor, image-02.png (venus.rise 1586), sunlit upper and upper-right limb (crop G; also image-03.png crops K, L): "highlights clip to a flat, washed-out pale yellow band along the outer sunlit disc before stopping abruptly at the circular edge" Fix named: "use smooth highlight tone-mapping rolloff so cloud contrast and limb brightening grade naturally without flat clipping"
- minor, image-02.png (venus.rise 1586), limb glare and dark-limb edge (crops D, G, H; also image-03.png crops K, O, Q): "crisp background stars sit unattenuated inside the blazing sunlit atmospheric glare and directly on the lower-left limb rim" Fix named: "mask stars completely behind the planet's opaque atmosphere radius and attenuate nearby stars beneath optical bloom and limb haze"
- minor, image-02.png (venus.rise 1586), starfield sky crops (crops C, E; also image-03.png crops M, R and image-04.png crops S, T, U, V, W, X): "most faint and mid-brightness stars are hard un-anti-aliased 1x1 square pixels that look blocky at native size and will shimmer in motion" Fix named: "render stars with a soft sub-pixel Gaussian or Airy point-spread function (around 1.5 to 2 pixels FWHM) instead of isolated single pixels"

Setup

4. S4 and S5 when I tell you that E1 and E2 are on main (the engine is being built now by another agent, handover_engineer, and proven on Mars first): merge main, opt in, remove your cover, bring light, exposure, haze, Sun and sky to one function of time through T, and run npm run handover for dive and rise.

What may not change: your hold segment and your terrain segment. The travel after your rise is not rendered again either: your rise must end in the pose, the speed and the look that travel starts with today. The timeline does not change.

Your stop

- The place is on the cloud tops of the close image, with the rays and the low Sun. Venus has no ground in view, so the cloud tops are the place: the cloud forms of the close view laid back onto the globe as plates, so that on the way down the camera flies to those very forms.
- The old cover was a bright haze 31 frames long, the longest in the film. It goes. A real haze layer may exist only as a thing in the scene: with structure, passing with parallax, the place seen through or round it, never one colour across the frame.
- What the critics wrote about the rise of the frozen build: "the rise pops: a close sunlit horizon becomes a half-globe, the sun leaves the frame, and the view direction changes in six frames"; "fast motion tears the limb into wavy accordion ridges instead of a smooth blur".

Working rules
- Your folders only: src/stops/venus/, assets-src/venus/, public/assets/venus/. Push only to agent/venus-place. Never main, never force, never rebase. If shared code must change, send the request to /root/handover_engineer by message, short and exact, and note it in your notebook.

## State 07:05, 23 Nov (mail 214, S1 and the first plate). Read this first after a compaction

Times UTC of this VM. Branch `agent/venus-place`. M1 due about 08:10. Looks every 30 minutes.

What exists now (all in my three folders):

- `assets-src/venus/place/horizon-points.json`: 70 traced horizon points of the approved close image (2816 x 1584).
- `assets-src/venus/place/horizon.py`: least squares fit of pitch, roll, dip at a given field of view. At 60 deg: pitch -21.47, roll 1.03 (its sign: up.x positive), dip 10.916, height 0.01843, rms 1.14 px, largest 3.9 px. At 50 deg the rms is 1.10 px, so the horizon alone does not fix the field of view. The relief's camera (pitch -21.48, dip 10.908, height 0.0184, up [0.016727, 0.930395, 0.366176]) is kept, so the terrain view does not change; against it the 70 points give rms 1.24 px, largest 4.4 px.
- `assets-src/venus/place/solve.ts` writes `src/stops/venus/place.json`: the twin at frame 1440 (plan frame and body frame), the point under it (body lon 59.42, lat 50.14), heading -73.4 deg from north (WNW), the aim of the optical axis (lon 55.07, lat 50.88, 2.86 deg ahead), horizon arc 10.908 deg, the footprint of the frame on the sphere (bottom centre 0.84 deg ahead).
- The twin formula: `Rot = turn(time) * terrainFrame`; position `Rot * (p - C) / R` with `C = -up * (R + 1)`, `R = 54.3478`; quaternion `Rot * q`. It keeps the world orientation of the close side, so the Sun and the sky stand where they stand on the close side. In `plan.ts` as `plan.twin(time, closePose)`.
- The point under the twin is 21.5 deg of arc from the plan's `spot` (the relief is built in the camera frame of the close image, which looks 21.5 deg below the level, and the terrain frame takes its y axis as the normal). So the place is not at the old spot: it lies in the twilight band right of the lit towers. In the hold it sits at 0.705, 0.409 of the frame (frame 1185), view cosine 0.62, the engine Sun 9.8 deg below its level (`assets-src/venus/place/where.ts` prints this for hold and dive frames). The old patch pyramid (centred on the spot, deepest level 14.25 deg wide) does not reach it with its deepest level.
- The Sun: the engine Sun seen by the twin at 1440 is at 0.7155, 0.2717 of the frame, the painted Sun of the approved image at 0.7470, 0.3177. Not an error of the solve: the film draws the engine Sun at 0.7153, 0.2718 in frame 1440 (measured from `shotAt`), the air dome hides it and the painted one is seen (old note, 16:00 entry on Sun size). For the handover both sides must show one Sun at T.
- Temporary probe: `globalThis.__venusProbe = { side: 'orbit' | 'terrain', tRel?, top?, vfov? }` read in `plan.ts` (`dive`, `coverAmount`, `veilAmount`, `release`). With it a dive frame is drawn from the close pose `terrain(tRel)` on the close side or from its twin on the globe side, with no cover; `top` gives a view from straight above the plate centre at that height with the twin's heading up. Unset, the film is as before. It is replaced by the engine's `forceSide` when E1 is merged. `assets-src/venus/place/check.ts` drives it.
- First check picture (`out/work/venus/place/check-f1349-terrain.png` and `-orbit.png`, draft, pose of 1440 drawn at dive frame 1349): the horizon of the globe side lies on the horizon of the close side (centre at 0.34 of the height in both, ends at 0.39 and 0.42 in both, read from the 960 px sheet). The globe there is dark (twilight), the engine Sun stands about 10 px of 960 from the painted Sun, the sky differs (other turn, other exposure).
- First plates, rough: `assets-src/venus/place/lay.py` lays the 2x upscale of the approved image back through the twin onto three gnomonic plates (`src/stops/venus/plates.json`: centre 2 deg ahead of the point under the twin, widths 24, 9 and 3.4 deg, 4096 px) and a cover mask (red, green, blue of `place-mask.png`). The frame covers 12, 47 and 82 percent of the three plates. Towers smear away from the camera, the haze near the horizon is laid on the ground: this is what S2 has to repaint.
- Globe shader: `uPlace0..2`, `uPlaceMask`, own frame (`uPlaceN/E/U`, body fixed, no drift), baked light: the plate is put over the lit, hazed colour as `inverseFilmic(plate)`, so from the twin it shows the close image's own values. `uPlaceOn` comes from the camera height (`plates.json near`: 0 above 1.6 radii, 1 below 0.4), so the hold (height 3.5) is not touched. 16 samplers in the globe shader now.

Next: check picture with the plates on, a view from above, looks push, commit, M1 report. Then S2 (repaint, colour match to the dim globe at the edges, deeper cover under and behind the camera from the relief canvas `out/work/venus/relief/canvas-1x.png`), S3.

S2, the design (decided 07:40):

- Four plates, not three, each with half the half tangent of its parent: 24, 12.133, 6.084 deg, and the deepest 3.4 deg; 4096 px; centre 1.5 deg ahead of the point under the twin (`plates.json`). Reason: G13 needs a step of 2 between levels, the last frames need 2.6e-5 rad per texel at the bottom of the frame (level c holds 1.07 texels per pixel there, d gives the margin).
- The mask texture is gone (16 samplers are the limit of the globe shader): a plate is a full square. The widest fades out on a circle (`rim` 0.3), the deeper ones on a square like the patches.
- The plates come in by time, not by height: `plates.json show` (dive shares 0.02 to 0.2, rise 0.8 to 0.98). Outside the dive and the rise of Venus `uPlaceOn` is 0.
- Exposure: `plates.json dusk`. The globe side is drawn `gain` times brighter (4.5) and turned by `tint` at the place, rising in the logarithm over dive shares 0.2 to 0.6 (`uDusk` in `globe.ts`, set in `set.ts`). The plates are drawn at the same exposure. Measured for the choice: the globe from straight above at gain 6 has the scene median 0.079, 0.071, 0.050; the laid back level c has 0.073, 0.055, 0.034.
- `lay.py` lays the 2x canvas (`out/work/venus/place/canvas-2x.png`: the 2x approved image plus the relief margins below and beside it) back and writes `laid`, `cover` (where the canvas has picture) and `trust` (where it is used as it is: camera at least 6 to 12 deg over the ground point, sine 0.1 to 0.2; border drawn in and softened). Shares used as they are: a 3.6, b 15.3, c 55.2, d 78.3 percent.
- `build.py`: `base` (the globe's own look at dusk in each plate frame, from `check.ts --tops 0.62,0.2 --on 0 --dusk 1 --format uhd` at frame 1300), `guide TAG` (trusted picture sharp, the rest a smooth underpainting: the low frequencies of the laid back picture carried outward, going to the globe's look between 5 and 9.5 deg from the centre; the finished child in the centre), `comp TAG TAKE` (take locked to the guide at sigma 40, trusted picture and child put back, 2x2 tiles cut), `plate TAG takes` (tiles joined by `patches/tiles.py`, trusted picture and child put back), `export`.
- Generation: composition take with the model of the relief margins (RECIPE, "Inputs made once"), tiles with the model of the dive patch tiles. Prompts in `assets-src/venus/place/prompts.json`. Level d: composition take 1 kept (the dark deck behind the camera grows out of the fan with no border). Tiles tr and br of d in work; tl and bl of d hold only trusted picture, so no tile is made for them (take 0).

## MAIL 235 (23 Nov, 09:51 VM time), from the Builder, word for word. Read this first after a compaction

"MAIL 235 from the Builder (/root). The engine's E2 is on main at c9c23b29. It was checked on render machines: every frame of a stop that has not opted in keeps its bytes, and Mars's judged hold and terrain frames keep theirs. Merge origin/main into your branch now.

What is new, and how a stop uses it, is in assets-src/shared/HANDOVER-NOTES.md, section "How a stop uses it". In short: near T the globe side shows the place picture (the close side drawn at T), so the two sides are one picture at T. StopPlan.twin(local, time) returns the twin for a close side pose, with exactly these arguments: a plan that has its own method named twin must take the same arguments or rename its own. Also closePose(kind, time) and place(kind, time), and per kind in MoveParams.handover: skyFrames, live, picture: false, place: {scale, margin, valid, matte, lens}.

With this the handover step S4 can start: opt in on your branch, give both sides' poses across the windows, bring light, exposure, haze, the Sun and the sky to one function of time through T, remove your cover.

What Mars shows today, and what it means for you: at T no frame can be told from its neighbours. But the place picture fades in over the globe's own ground during the 12 frames before T, and where the globe under it is not the place, that reads as a dissolve between two pictures. Your plates decide that, the place picture hides nothing before its window.

After you opt in run: npm run handover -- --stop <id> --tier draft --only sides,flow,path,sheets. Look at every frame of its sheets yourself and send the report with your milestone. Do not answer this mail, go on."

## MAIL 258 (23 Nov, about 11:54 VM time), from the Builder to every stop builder, word for word. No answer needed

"MAIL 258 from the Builder (/root) to every stop builder.
Main moved to da69509a. New in the globe shader and the loader, all off unless your stop asks (notes: assets-src/shared/GLOBE-NOTES.md):
- `ctx.texture(path, {srgb: false, channels: 1})`: a one channel texture, a quarter of the memory. Use it for every mask and height map you load, if your set is near its memory limit.
- `PatchSet.own?: boolean[]`: a patch level with its own light (a plate that already carries the light of the close view is not lit a second time).
- `PatchSet.fadeMinor?: boolean`: a level fades in by its fine axis on screen, so a level seen at a grazing angle does not come in as a hard strip.
MAX_PATCHES is 10.
No frame of main changed (115 draft frames with the same bytes). Merge origin/main into your branch at your next commit. Nothing else changes for you: go on with the list of my last mail to you."

## MAIL 265 (23 Nov, 12:36 UTC), from the Builder, high priority, word for word (the parts for other builders left out are marked)

"MAIL 265 from the Builder (/root), 12:36 UTC. E3 is on main.

Main is at 03510ddd. It holds the handover engine of agent/engine-handover 0d14d1cf: the steady descent in the generic path, its mirror for the rise, the sky floor, the sidestep option. Proven by me before it was taken: 105 of 105 draft frames outside the Mars dive and rise keep their bytes, 2325 and 2580 at the final tier equal the kept stills, four review tier frames equal main, 155 tests green.

What it means for a stop that has opted in and uses the generic path (read `assets-src/shared/HANDOVER-NOTES.md`, section "E3 as built"):
1. Your dive and rise fly another line after the merge. Height falls by a near constant ratio per frame, the camera comes down a line of sight to the place and levels into the glide of your close view. The low end of the path is the close pose at T carried on with its own speed and spin over `coastFrames`.
2. If the new line does not fit your stop yet, keep the old one for now with `handover: { dive: { path: { turnFall: 0.3, level: false } } }` (and the same under `rise`). Say so in your report, with the reason.
